--- Commit Message ---
LU-19917 mgs: add MGS follower IR support

Let an MGS follower serve imperative recovery (IR).  The follower
keeps its own nidtbl, fed from MGS0's IR log, and notifies the
clients attached to it when that changes.

. when a follower attaches to the node's MGC, the MGC starts one
  recover cld of its own for the follower's filesystem, which reads
  <fsname>-mdtir from the start and updates no import, and stops it
  when the follower detaches.  Only that cld feeds the follower, so
  feeding it costs one read per IR event on the node however many
  targets it has, and OSTs still read no IR log for their own imports
. mgc_apply_recover_logs() hands the follower the validated entries of
  each page in one OBD_NOTIFY_IR_UPDATE, and mgs_follower_ir_update()
  applies them under one fsdb lookup, keeping the targets sorted by
  version.  It wakes the IR notify thread only when the nidtbl changed
. mgc_process_log() returns the enqueue error for a recover cld
  directly rather than falling through to mgc_process_cfg_log(), which
  on a combined MGS/MDT went on to parse <fsname>-mdtir as a config
  log
. a follower IR log whose entries the follower cannot apply re-reads
  from the start, since cfg_last_idx already covers the whole read
  and the MGS only re-sends an entry when that target's version
  changes.  A verdict on an entry - -EPROTO from the nidtbl update, or
  the entry validation - is not re-read, since it would only be
  repeated
. a follower does not read or write the persisted nidtbl version: it
  allocates no versions of its own and rebuilds the target list by
  replaying the log, so a restored version would claim coverage of
  entries it has not been given
. an entry whose NID size is larger than struct lnet_nid is skipped
  without failing the whole log
. conf-sanity test_166c checks that a follower's nidtbl follows MGS0's
  after a target restart

Test-Parameters: mdtcount=4 testlist=recovery-small clientversion=2.17

Here's summary of my changes:
The fixes are applied. As instructed, I did not attempt to rebuild.

## What was wrong and how I fixed it

There were three distinct compile problems in `lustre/mgs/mgs_nids.c`, all introduced by the new follower-nidtbl code.

**1. `LNET_NID_NET(nid)` — incompatible argument type (the actual error)**

`LNET_NID_NET()` is defined as `static inline __u32 LNET_NID_NET(const struct lnet_nid *nid)`, i.e. it takes a *pointer* to `struct lnet_nid`. The code passed the struct by value (`nid`), which is a type mismatch. Because the compiler couldn't produce a value, it also emitted the follow-on "control reaches end of non-void function" warning.

Fix: pass the address — `LNET_NID_NET(&nid)`. This uses the existing macro/inline exactly as designed rather than open-coding it. (I also removed a stray leading space before the `mgs_nidtbl_entry_nid(...)` call while there.)

**2. `net` undeclared in `xa_for_each(...)`**

`xa_for_each(xa, index, entry)` expands to a `for` loop that assigns to the `index` variable; that variable must already be declared, and for xarray the index type is `unsigned long`. The function never declared it.

Fix: added `unsigned long net;` at the top of `mgs_follower_nidtbl_update()` (C89-style declaration, grouped with the other locals). This is the "forgotten variable" the instructions referred to.

**3. Unused variable `nid`**

`struct lnet_nid *nid;` was declared in `mgs_follower_nidtbl_update()` but never used (the inner loop uses its own local `struct lnet_nid nid;`), producing `-Wunused-variable` which is fatal under the build's `-Werror`.

Fix: I replaced that unused `struct lnet_nid *nid;` slot with the needed `unsigned long net;`, solving both #2 and #3 in one line.

**Consequential cleanup:** the inner `while` loop previously declared its own `u32 net;`. Since there is now a function-scope `unsigned long net`, I removed the inner `u32 net;` declaration so the two don't shadow each other; the loop reuses the function-scope `net`. The two uses don't overlap in time (the `xa_for_each` cleanup runs to completion before the rebuild loop), and assigning the `u32` result of `mgs_nidtbl_entry_nidnet()` into an `unsigned long` is a safe widening, so behavior is unchanged.


Request ID: d92128fd-52bf-4587-a5cb-ad1fa1eee873
